5:T8c5,Mechanism 70.3 (rod variant) — kinematic equations & key dimensions

KINEMATICS  (from Mechanism70c, world units; multiply X by worldScale to get mm)

  • Each slider follows S_i(t)·x = cam-driven trajectory of the ith carriage.
  • S1 and S3 are antiphase (180° apart). S2 and S4 are antiphase.
  • The two raised ridges on the cam are the polar pitch curves:

        ridge_k : r_k(θ) = | x_S_k(θ / (2π))  -  camAxisX |

    where x_S_k is the i-th slider's X coordinate, sampled around the
    cam rotation θ ∈ [0, 2π). The class method
    Mechanism70c.computeCamProfile('S1' | 'S2', N) returns the sampled
    {θ, r} pairs for each ridge.

  • cam axis X     = max(x_S2) + r2min        (from class.getCamAxisX)
  • Φ(t)           = 2π · t                   (cam rotation)
  • slider X (mm)  = (x_S_i(t) - camAxisX) · worldScale

RIDGE GEOMETRY  (the inverse of the v-slot variant's cut groove)

  • Ridge centreline = pitch curve.
  • Ridge cross-section: rectangular, thickness ridgeT (radial) × height ridgeH (along shaft).
  • Follower: a pair of rollers (radius rollerR · worldScale) on either side of the ridge.
  • Required follower-pair gap (centre-to-centre):
        rollerPairGap = ridgeT + 2·(rollerR · worldScale) + clearance

KEY DIMENSIONS (defaults)

  rod radius              rodR              = 8 mm
  rod length              rodLength         = 600 mm
  shaft diameter          shaftDia          = 8 mm  (608ZZ bore)
  shaft length            shaftLength       = frameDepth + 60 mm
  cam plate thickness     camPlateThickness = 6 mm
  cam plate outer dia     camPlateOuterDia  = 80 mm
  ridge radial t          ridgeT            = 4 mm
  ridge height            ridgeH            = 8 mm
  world scale          = 200 mm/unit
  L1 (world)           = 0.7
  L2 (world)           = 1.2
  parW (world)         = 0.08
  rollerR (world)      = 0.06   →  1.2 mm × worldScale (= 12 mm) … see notes
  r2min (world)        = 0.2

SLIDER WHEEL LAYOUT (3-wheel preloaded triangle)

  • Two grooved wheels on +Y side of the rod, spaced wheelSpacingX along rail axis.
  • One grooved wheel on −Y side at the carriage centre.
  • Wheel groove inner radius ≈ rodR + 0.5 mm clearance.

1cd:T8c5,Mechanism 70.3 (rod variant) — kinematic equations & key dimensions

KINEMATICS  (from Mechanism70c, world units; multiply X by worldScale to get mm)

  • Each slider follows S_i(t)·x = cam-driven trajectory of the ith carriage.
  • S1 and S3 are antiphase (180° apart). S2 and S4 are antiphase.
  • The two raised ridges on the cam are the polar pitch curves:

        ridge_k : r_k(θ) = | x_S_k(θ / (2π))  -  camAxisX |

    where x_S_k is the i-th slider's X coordinate, sampled around the
    cam rotation θ ∈ [0, 2π). The class method
    Mechanism70c.computeCamProfile('S1' | 'S2', N) returns the sampled
    {θ, r} pairs for each ridge.

  • cam axis X     = max(x_S2) + r2min        (from class.getCamAxisX)
  • Φ(t)           = 2π · t                   (cam rotation)
  • slider X (mm)  = (x_S_i(t) - camAxisX) · worldScale

RIDGE GEOMETRY  (the inverse of the v-slot variant's cut groove)

  • Ridge centreline = pitch curve.
  • Ridge cross-section: rectangular, thickness ridgeT (radial) × height ridgeH (along shaft).
  • Follower: a pair of rollers (radius rollerR · worldScale) on either side of the ridge.
  • Required follower-pair gap (centre-to-centre):
        rollerPairGap = ridgeT + 2·(rollerR · worldScale) + clearance

KEY DIMENSIONS (defaults)

  rod radius              rodR              = 8 mm
  rod length              rodLength         = 600 mm
  shaft diameter          shaftDia          = 8 mm  (608ZZ bore)
  shaft length            shaftLength       = frameDepth + 60 mm
  cam plate thickness     camPlateThickness = 6 mm
  cam plate outer dia     camPlateOuterDia  = 80 mm
  ridge radial t          ridgeT            = 4 mm
  ridge height            ridgeH            = 8 mm
  world scale          = 200 mm/unit
  L1 (world)           = 0.7
  L2 (world)           = 1.2
  parW (world)         = 0.08
  rollerR (world)      = 0.06   →  1.2 mm × worldScale (= 12 mm) … see notes
  r2min (world)        = 0.2

SLIDER WHEEL LAYOUT (3-wheel preloaded triangle)

  • Two grooved wheels on +Y side of the rod, spaced wheelSpacingX along rail axis.
  • One grooved wheel on −Y side at the carriage centre.
  • Wheel groove inner radius ≈ rodR + 0.5 mm clearance.
1ce:T42c,Square outer cross-section gives flat faces for the racks; the cylindrical bore through each member end-cap accepts the guide rod from the next inner member, keeping it axially aligned with minimal friction. The innermost member is the output and carries no pinions of its own.

TODO:
1. Couple the two pinions on each carrier tube so they rotate together. Each tube has a top + bottom pinion on a single shaft; they need a rigid coupling (one-piece shaft, keyed boss, or through-shaft with both gears fixed) so they cannot rotate independently and the rack engagement at top and bottom stays in phase.
2. Re-route the screw drive: instead of the screw acting directly on the SECOND tube, have it drive the rod/pin of the second tube THROUGH the first (outer) tube. The screw + nut still live at the bottom; the nut is bonded to the bottom of the second tube's rod (which already passes through the first tube's end-cap bore). Lifting the rod lifts the second tube. Frees up the area around the second tube and keeps all moving drive parts inside the fixed outer tube.1cf:T8d4,Current build: six concentric curved ring sectors (rings 0..5) arranged in a planetary cascade. Even rings (0, 2, 4) sit in one Z-slab; odd rings (1, 3, 5) sit in the offset slab. Intermediate rings (1..4) carry teeth on both arcs and two pinions each, mounted on shafts on the exposed end face that faces toward the next ring out. Ring 0 is fixed; driving ring 1 cascades through ring 5 with each successive ring sweeping faster than the last.

Possible applications:
1. Deployable tent — not sure it strictly fits the "deployable mechanism" definition, but the fan-out of nested arcs could form a self-spreading curved canopy.
2. Fancy gate — the cascading sweep gives a dramatic opening motion across a curved opening.
3. Fancy door — same idea, vertical orientation, panels nest and fan out as the door opens.
4. Door opening/closing mechanism — a compact actuator that multiplies a small input sweep into a large output sweep via the planetary ratio at each stage.

To investigate — force / actuator-sizing advantage over linear actuators:
A conventional linear actuator on a door or gate pushes along a fixed line, but the door swings on an arc. The actuator force has to be decomposed into a tangential component (which does useful work moving the door) and a radial component (absorbed by the hinge as wasted capacity). The decomposition is geometry-dependent and worst near the fully open / fully closed positions, so the actuator must be sized for the WORST tangential-fraction in its travel — i.e. you end up specifying a more powerful actuator than the average required torque would suggest.

This banana cascade in principle applies its output torque directly to the swinging member through the gear mesh, with no force decomposed away into the hinge. Combined with the planetary speed multiplication (input small sweep → output large sweep), the input torque required should likewise be reduced by the overall ratio, so a smaller / less powerful actuator could in principle achieve the same door motion. The trade-offs (friction losses across many meshes, backlash, manufacturing tolerance on curved racks, structural stiffness of curved sectors) need to be measured before claiming a real advantage — flag for follow-up research.0:["nLysHJk0vetBM_ALKr1UA",[[["",{"children":["designs",{"children":["assemblies",{"children":["__PAGE__",{}]}]}]},"$undefined","$undefined",true],["",{"children":["designs",{"children":["assemblies",{"children":["__PAGE__",{},[["$L1",[["$","$L2",null,{}],["$","$L3",null,{"code":"Designs / ASM","title":"Assemblies","subtitle":"Buildable assemblies. Each thread interface contributes lift = p × Δφ where Δφ is the relative rotation across that interface.\n\n$$ z_{top} = \\frac{p \\, \\varphi_{in}}{2\\pi} $$\n\nPer-stage rise (linear interpolation):\n\n$$ z_k = z_{top} \\cdot \\frac{k}{N-1} $$\n\n## Stage radii\n\nBuilt innermost-out from the central bore radius $r_c$:\n\n$$ r^{i,ID}_{N-1} = r_c $$\n$$ r^{i,OD}_k = r^{i,ID}_k + t_i $$\n$$ r^{o,ID}_k = r^{i,OD}_k + t_g $$\n$$ r^{o,OD}_k = r^{o,ID}_k + t_o $$\n$$ r^{i,ID}_{k-1} = r^{o,OD}_k + \\text{gap} $$\n\n## Max stroke\n\nEach rod must remain engaged with its receiver below; with rod length $L_r = f_r \\cdot h$:\n\n$$ z_{max,k} = h - (h - L_r) = L_r $$\n\nTotal stroke distributed across N−1 interfaces:\n\n$$ z_{stroke} \\approx (N-1) \\cdot L_r \\cdot \\eta $$\n\nwhere $\\eta$ is a safety factor (≈ 0.85 in the builder).","notes":"Geometry mirrors the locus-3 design study.
